\subsubsection{Cross-ISA Multi-Node Portability, Prediction-Noise Stability, and Planner/OOD Safety Gate Summary}
Full per-node, noise-drift, and planner comparison matrices are provided in Additional File~1 (Tables~S8--S11 and Figs.~S7--S8) and establish three operational scope boundaries:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.5em,itemsep=2pt]
    \item \textbf{Controlled $2\times 2$ Engine-Version Isolation, Three-Node Clean-Room Replication, and Few-Shot Cross-Node Calibration Curve ($m \in \{0,1,3,5,10,15\}$ Queries; Additional File~1, Table~S11):} While all primary Node~1 benchmarks use the production software stack (\textbf{Manifest~A}: \texttt{DuckDB v2.0.0}, \texttt{Polars v2.1.0}, \texttt{DataFusion v55.0.0}), isolating pure hardware instruction-set architecture (\texttt{x86\_64} vs.\ \texttt{ARM64}) from software release differences requires a secondary cross-ISA parity virtual environment (\textbf{Manifest~B} [\texttt{.venv\_pinned\_parity}]: \texttt{duckdb==1.4.5}, \texttt{polars==1.36.1}, \texttt{datafusion==50.1.0}) pinned identically at $8$ threads across \texttt{Node-1-x86} ($\mathbf{h}_{\text{hw}}^{(1)}=[9.0,4.0,5.69,8,25.6]^{\top}$) and \texttt{Node-3-ARM64-M1} (Apple M1 $8\text{C}/8\text{T}$, $8\text{ GB}$ unified memory, $\mathbf{h}_{\text{hw}}^{(3)}=[8.0,0,0,8,68.3]^{\top}$). This clean-room multi-machine replication confirms $\text{EX}=1.000$ ($\text{FP64}$ error $\le 5.74\times 10^{-16}$) and reproduces the Tier~L4 Polars-over-DuckDB window-sorting crossover across instruction sets ($66.41$ vs.\ $70.75\text{ ms}$ on M1; $30$-query LOGO-CV on M1 Node~3B achieves $\mathbf{96.67\%}$ Top-1 [$29/30$] at $\mathbf{29.44\text{ ms}}$ net vs.\ $28.42\text{ ms}$ Oracle). On the independent $16$-core/$22$-thread \texttt{x86\_64} workstation (Node~2, $\mathbf{h}_{\text{hw}}^{(2)}=[9.0,0,0,22,42.7]^{\top}$), $30$-query LOGO-CV achieves $\mathbf{86.67\%}$ Top-1 ($26/30$, $62.05\text{ ms}$ net in Node~2A). Crucially, evaluating our Leave-One-Hardware-Node-Out few-shot log-ratio calibration curve (\texttt{v8\_5\_cross\_node\_calibration\_curve\_1\_3\_5\_10q.csv}) when migrating the locked $8$-thread Node~1 model to $22$-thread Node~2 shows monotonic convergence across $m \in \{0,1,3,5,10,15\}$ calibration queries: $m=0$ zero-shot (Node~2B: $40.00\%$ Top-1, $70.00\%$ Top-2, $54.81\text{ ms}$ net, $52.14\%$ regret, trailing Polars at $47.54\text{ ms}$ because thread scaling shifts relative CPU operator speedups) $\rightarrow m=1$ query ($0.18\text{ s}$ one-time profiling: $42.67\%$ Top-1, $52.18\text{ ms}$ net, $44.82\%$ regret) $\rightarrow m=3$ queries ($0.54\text{ s}$: $45.33\%$ Top-1, $49.85\text{ ms}$ net, $38.90\%$ regret) $\rightarrow m=5$ queries ($0.91\text{ s}$: $46.67\%$ Top-1, $48.92\text{ ms}$ net, $36.15\%$ regret) $\rightarrow m=10$ queries ($1.82\text{ s}$: $48.00\%$ Top-1, $48.54\text{ ms}$ net, $35.12\%$ regret) $\rightarrow m=15$ queries ($5$ seeds, $2.74\text{ s}$ profiling, $0.00\text{ ms}$ added online overhead: $48.00\%$ Top-1, $78.33\%$ Top-2, $\mathbf{48.37\text{ ms}}$ net, $34.68\%$ regret), while full in-domain retraining on Node~2 ($72$ training queries/fold in Node~2C) reaches $\mathbf{35.11\text{ ms}}$ ($\mathbf{97.33\%}$ Top-1 [$73/75$] due to the wide latency gap between Polars/DuckDB and DataFusion on $22$ threads).
    \item \textbf{Decision Stability Under Prediction Noise and Drift-Triggered Recalibration (Additional File~1, Table~S8 \& Table~S14):} Under $\pm 10\%$ and $\pm 20\%$ multiplicative cost-prediction noise ($1{,}000$ Monte Carlo trials), Rule~U1's $12\%$ PCIe guard maintains $94.22\%$ and $88.00\%$ routing decision stability (reducing engine flip churn by $-21.4\%$ and $-14.6\%$ vs.\ unguarded $\arg\min$, with $61.2\%$ of flips occurring between near-tied CPU engines within $\le 15\text{ ms}$). Across a 5-epoch non-stationary stream ($375$ queries spanning query-mix shift, selectivity expansion, and Zipfian skew $s=1.4$), monitoring Population Stability Index ($\text{PSI}>0.25$) and conformal coverage ($\text{PI}<80\%$) triggers recalibration only on true distribution shifts ($0.0\%$ false-alarm rate, $412.4\text{ ms}$ total overhead vs.\ $9{,}200\text{ ms}$ for periodic full retraining) and across $180$ fault-injection trials ($6\times 30$ scenarios) achieves $180/180$ ($100.0\%$, Wilson 95\% CI $[97.91\%, 100.00\%]$) graceful fallback recovery in $0.66\text{ ms}$ mean detection time.
    \item \textbf{Cross-Engine SQL AST vs.\ Single-Engine \texttt{EXPLAIN} and OOD Unseen-Operator Fallback (Additional File~1, Table~S9):} Feeding BigQuery array/unnest SQL (Tier~L5) into untranspiled \texttt{DuckDB EXPLAIN} fails binder validation on $15/75$ queries ($78.77\text{ ms}$ fallback net, $362.15\%$ P95 regret), while a Plan-Graph Neural Cost Encoder (MLP-$64\times 32$, $5.47\text{ ms}$ overhead) yields $58.90\text{ ms}$ net. Under 5-fold Leave-One-Tier-Out CV (LOTO-CV), holding out an entire SQL operator family degrades unguarded tree routing to $85.42\text{ ms}$ ($348.0\%$ P95 regret, trailing Polars at $82.47\text{ ms}$); enforcing our zero-cost OOD structural domain check ($W(q)>\max_{\text{train}}W$, $U(q)>\max_{\text{train}}U$, or $J(q)>\max_{\text{train}}J \rightarrow$ Arrow CPU fallback) reduces LOTO-CV net latency to $\mathbf{76.18\text{ ms}}$ ($p_{\text{seed}}=0.0312$ vs.\ Polars).
\end{enumerate}

% === BEGIN V9 SECTION 3.5 EXTENDED EXTERNAL GENERALIZATION ON TPC-DS-DERIVED (N=30) AND BIRD-INSPIRED (N=20) ===
\subsection{Extended External Generalization Across Five Cross-Schema Benchmarks (\texorpdfstring{$N_{\text{ext}}=116$}{Next=116} Queries Including TPC-DS-Derived and BIRD-Inspired Schemas)}
\label{sec:v9_sec3_5_tpcds_bird_external_validation}
\label{sec:v9_external_130_validation}
To rigorously validate that \textbf{AST-Route} generalizes to complex analytical snowflake topologies and enterprise SQL patterns not designed in our core TPC-H study, we expanded our external cross-schema validation from $66$ queries across three suites---all $33/33$ ($100\%$) canonical join-order template families (\texttt{1a}--\texttt{33a}) of IMDB JOB ($N=33$~\cite{leis2015job}, 1 representative query per template to prevent intra-template leakage), all $13/13$ ($100\%$) standard flights of SSB ($N=13$~\cite{oneil2009ssb}), and the $20$ standard ANSI-compatible analytical queries from the 43-query ClickBench suite ($N=20$~\cite{clickbench2022}) that execute with exact multiset parity ($\text{EX}=1.000$) across all four local engines without ClickHouse-proprietary SQL extensions---by provisioning and executing two additional schema-faithful external workloads: a TPC-DS-derived multi-channel snowflake workload ($N=30$) and a BIRD-inspired synthetic financial workload ($N=20$), bringing total external validation to $\mathbf{N_{\text{ext}}=116}$ \textbf{queries across five cross-schema suites} ($N_{\text{eval}}=213$ benchmark queries overall, or $228$ including prospective Seed~\texttt{S6} holdout queries). All $50$ additional external queries were executed live ($W=1$ warm-up, $R=3$ timed repetitions/query, $150$ timed trials/engine) on the primary \texttt{x86\_64} + \texttt{NVIDIA RTX 2050} workstation (Node~1) across the unified software stack (\texttt{DuckDB v2.0.0}, \texttt{Polars v2.1.0}, \texttt{Apache DataFusion v55.0.0}, and \texttt{PyTorch 2.6.0+cu124}, and cross-checked in our version-pinned cross-ISA parity environment \texttt{duckdb==1.4.5}, \texttt{polars==1.36.1}, \texttt{datafusion==50.1.0} in Additional File~1, Table~S11), with $100\%$ verified multiset row parity and IEEE-754 \texttt{FP64} checksum equivalence ($\text{EX}=1.000$, maximum relative error $< 10^{-9}$ across all $200$ query--engine pairs). We emphasize that these two suites are schema-faithful derived/synthetic analytical workloads constructed to test multi-channel snowflake rollups and conditional ratio joins under controlled cross-engine execution parity rather than unmodified official benchmark queries; furthermore, while conservative Regime~O1 cold-SQL parsing ($+4.15\text{ ms}$) represents $\sim 17\%$ of net latency on $\sim 20\text{ ms}$ in-memory external queries ($24.18\text{--}24.39\text{ ms}$ net, still $1.4\times\text{--}3.0\times$ faster than static CPU baselines), template-cached Regimes~O2 ($+0.67\text{ ms}$) and~O3 ($+0.08\text{ ms}$) reduce router overhead to $<3.3\%$ and $<0.4\%$ ($20.72\text{--}20.91\text{ ms}$ and $20.13\text{--}20.32\text{ ms}$ net). As detailed in Table~\ref{tab:v9_sec3_5_tpcds_bird_external_validation}, we evaluate strict zero-shot transfer ($\mathcal{R}_{\text{GBR-9D}}$ trained strictly on the $75$ TPC-H queries at $\text{SF}=1\text{ GB}$ with zero external labels), the four-model structural progression (\textbf{M1--M4}), Level~2 12D catalog-sketch augmentation ($\hat{\psi}_{\text{join}}(q) = \log_{10}(1 + |R_{\text{fact}}|\cdot\hat{\sigma}(q)\cdot J(q))$ from Parquet footers), and Level~3 nested 5-fold template-disjoint cross-validation:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.5em,itemsep=2pt]
    \item \textbf{TPC-DS-Derived Multi-Channel Snowflake Workload ($N=30$ Queries Across 11 Tables, Part~A):} Following the multi-channel snowflake schema of TPC-DS~\cite{nambiar2006tpcds}, we provisioned 11 ZSTD-compressed Parquet tables (\texttt{store\_sales} $1.50\times 10^6$ rows, \texttt{catalog\_sales} $5.00\times 10^5$, \texttt{web\_sales} $4.00\times 10^5$, \texttt{store\_returns} $2.50\times 10^5$, \texttt{customer} $1.00\times 10^5$, \texttt{customer\_address} $5.00\times 10^4$, \texttt{item} $2.00\times 10^4$, \texttt{customer\_demographics} $1.00\times 10^4$, \texttt{date\_dim} $1826$, \texttt{promotion} $500$, and \texttt{store} $200$; $2.83\times 10^6$ total rows) and synthesized 30 schema-faithful analytical queries spanning five structural families: (F1) selective multi-channel store/catalog/web/returns range lookups ($J=1\text{--}2$), (F2) 4-way to 6-way store-sales snowflake rollups ($J=4\text{--}6$), (F3) 1-stage and 2-stage CTE demographic/promotion rollups ($J=2\text{--}6, S=1\text{--}2$), (F4) partitioned item/region/loyalty window cumulative frames ($J=4\text{--}5, S=2, W=1\text{--}2$), and (F5) 6-way snowflake joins with 3-branch conditional \texttt{CASE} margin matrices ($J=6, S=1, C=3$; overall mean $\bar{J}=4.27$, mean $\overline{\text{CSCI}}=31.05$). Under strict zero-shot transfer, unweighted \textbf{M1 (Raw AST 7D)} achieves $63.33\%$ Top-1 ($19/30$, $21.31\text{ ms}$ exec, $28.09\%$ mean regret, $137.74\%$ P95 regret, $\text{ECE}=32.33\%$) and \textbf{M2 (AST + Selectivity 8D)} achieves $86.67\%$ Top-1 ($26/30$, $20.93\text{ ms}$ exec, $5.29\%$ mean regret, $38.80\%$ P95 regret, $\text{ECE}=23.47\%$). Adding $\text{CSCI}(q)$ in \textbf{M3 (Canonical CSCI 9D)} and \textbf{M4 (Learned CSCI 9D)} lifts strict zero-shot accuracy to $\mathbf{93.33\%}$ \textbf{Top-1} ($\mathbf{28/30}$) and $\mathbf{96.67\%}$ \textbf{Top-2} ($29/30$), achieving $\mathbf{20.05\text{ ms}}$ execution latency ($24.20\text{ ms}$ net vs.\ $19.88\text{ ms}$ Oracle), $\mathbf{0.00\%}$ median regret, $\mathbf{1.61\%}$ mean regret, $\mathbf{8.76\%}$ P95 tail regret, and $\text{ECE}=\mathbf{8.80\%}$---outperforming Always DataFusion ($52.62\text{ ms}$) by $\mathbf{2.62\times}$ ($p_{\text{Holm}}=8.01e-07$), Always DuckDB ($59.49\text{ ms}$) by $\mathbf{2.97\times}$ ($p_{\text{Holm}}=3.81e-06$), Always Polars ($72.33\text{ ms}$) by $\mathbf{3.61\times}$ ($p_{\text{Holm}}=1.12e-07$), and compressing P95 tail regret by $\mathbf{33.79\times}$ vs.\ Always GPU Cold ($295.98\%\rightarrow 8.76\%$, $p_{\text{Holm}}=1.17e-02$) and by $\mathbf{19.68\times}$ vs.\ Rule-Based Routing ($172.40\%\rightarrow 8.76\%$). Augmenting with the static 12D base-table join-fanout sketch (\textbf{Level~2}) reaches $\mathbf{96.67\%}$ \textbf{Top-1} ($\mathbf{29/30}$) and $\mathbf{100.00\%}$ \textbf{Top-2} at $\mathbf{20.03\text{ ms}}$ ($0.53\%$ mean regret, $0.00\%$ P95 regret, $\text{ECE}=6.73\%$), while nested 5-fold CV (\textbf{Level~3}) reaches $\mathbf{100.00\%}$ \textbf{Top-1} ($30/30$, $19.88\text{ ms}$).
    \item \textbf{BIRD-Inspired Synthetic Financial Workload ($N=20$ Queries Across 6 Tables, Part~B):} Inspired by the financial relational schema and division/ratio SQL idioms in the BIRD benchmark~\cite{li2023bird}, we provisioned 6 synthetic relational tables (\texttt{card\_transactions} $1.40\times 10^6$ rows, \texttt{loan\_contracts} $3.00\times 10^5$, \texttt{accounts} $1.20\times 10^5$, \texttt{clients} $1.20\times 10^5$, \texttt{merchants} $1.00\times 10^4$, and \texttt{districts} $500$; $1.95\times 10^6$ total rows, mean $\bar{J}=3.35$, mean $\overline{\text{CSCI}}=29.35$) across four enterprise query families: (B1) selective card fraud, foreign-fee, and delinquent loan lookups ($J=1\text{--}2$), (B2) 4-way enterprise conditional ratio joins across region, credit risk, and MCC groups ($J=4, C=2$), (B3) 2-stage CTE merchant fraud and overdraft cohort rollups ($J=4, S=2, C=3$), and (B4) partitioned window cohort and cumulative balance analytics ($J=4, S=2, W=2, C=2$). While \textbf{M1 (Raw AST 7D)} ($20.00\%$ Top-1, $67.32\text{ ms}$, $315.65\%$ mean regret) and \textbf{M2 (AST + Selectivity 8D)} ($45.00\%$ Top-1, $43.79\text{ ms}$, $100.49\%$ mean regret, $236.67\%$ P95 regret) fail to capture the composite join--window--branch interaction on enterprise financial ratios, \textbf{M3 (Canonical CSCI 9D)} and \textbf{M4 (Learned CSCI 9D)} achieve $\mathbf{90.00\%}$ \textbf{Zero-Shot Top-1 Accuracy} ($\mathbf{18/20}$), $\mathbf{20.24\text{ ms}}$ execution latency ($24.39\text{ ms}$ net vs.\ $19.79\text{ ms}$ Oracle), $\mathbf{0.00\%}$ median regret, $\mathbf{9.03\%}$ mean regret, and $\mathbf{80.69\%}$ P95 regret---outperforming Always Polars ($59.27\text{ ms}$) by $\mathbf{2.93\times}$, Always DuckDB ($63.14\text{ ms}$) by $\mathbf{3.12\times}$, Always DataFusion ($69.68\text{ ms}$) by $\mathbf{3.44\times}$, and Rule-Based Routing ($44.28\text{ ms}$) by $\mathbf{2.19\times}$ ($p_{\text{Holm}}\le 0.0045$), while cutting Always GPU Cold P95 tail regret by $\mathbf{3.71\times}$ ($299.68\%\rightarrow 80.69\%$). Adding the 12D base-table cardinality sketch (\textbf{Level~2}) lifts zero-label accuracy to $\mathbf{95.00\%}$ \textbf{Top-1} ($19/20$, $20.00\text{ ms}$, $3.98\%$ mean regret, $3.98\%$ P95 regret), and nested 5-fold CV (\textbf{Level~3}) reaches $\mathbf{100.00\%}$ \textbf{Top-1} and $\mathbf{100.00\%}$ \textbf{Top-2} ($20/20$) at $\mathbf{19.79\text{ ms}}$ ($0.00\%$ regret).
    \item \textbf{Strict Four-Regime Cross-Schema Generalization Protocol Across All Five External Suites ($N_{\text{ext}}=116$ Queries, Part~C):} To eliminate any ambiguity between zero-shot transfer and post-hoc schema calibration, Table~\ref{tab:v9_sec3_5_tpcds_bird_external_validation} Part~C strictly separates four evaluation regimes across all five external datasets ($33\text{ JOB} + 13\text{ SSB} + 20\text{ ClickBench} + 30\text{ TPC-DS-derived} + 20\text{ BIRD-inspired} = 116$ queries) and reports all six diagnostic dimensions: cost prediction error (Median P50 and P90 Q-Error), Top-1/Top-2 accuracy, execution and net latency, relative regret (Mean and P95 \%), absolute regret (Mean and P95 ms), and uncertainty abstention rate (\%). Under \textbf{Regime~1 (Strict Zero-Shot 9D, 0 External Labels, No Sketch)}, the locked TPC-H-trained $\mathcal{R}_{\text{GBR-9D}}$ model achieves $\mathbf{69.83\%}$ \textbf{Top-1} ($\mathbf{81/116}$), $\mathbf{96.55\%}$ \textbf{Top-2} ($\mathbf{112/116}$), median cost Q-Error $=1.189$ (P90 $=1.812$), $\mathbf{14.95\text{ ms}}$ execution ($\mathbf{19.10\text{ ms}}$ net), $27.95\%$ mean relative regret ($1.30\text{ ms}$ mean / $5.31\text{ ms}$ P95 absolute regret), and $21.55\%$ abstention rate ($25/116$ queries abstaining to safe Arrow CPU). Crucially, under \textbf{Regime~1-SQL (Strict Zero-Shot 9D Pure Native-SQL Pool, $0\%$ GPU Prototype, 0 External Labels)}, routing strictly among standard SQL engines (\texttt{DuckDB}, \texttt{Polars}, \texttt{DataFusion}) achieves $\mathbf{82.76\%}$ \textbf{Top-1} ($\mathbf{96/116}$), $\mathbf{98.28\%}$ \textbf{Top-2} ($\mathbf{114/116}$), median Q-Error $=1.148$ (P90 $=1.615$), $\mathbf{16.99\text{ ms}}$ execution ($\mathbf{21.14\text{ ms}}$ net), $\mathbf{7.42\%}$ mean relative regret ($28.40\%$ P95), and only $\mathbf{1.18\text{ ms}}$ mean absolute regret ($4.25\text{ ms}$ P95 abs), proving strong zero-shot cross-schema routing purely across production SQL engines without the GPU prototype. Under \textbf{Regime~2 (Static Catalog Sketch 12D, 0 External Runtime Labels, $+10.18\text{ KB}$ Base-Table Sketch)}, augmenting $\mathbf{x}_q$ with static base-table string/skew/fanout sketches ($\hat{\sigma}_{\text{str}}, \hat{\zeta}_{\text{skew}}, \hat{\psi}_{\text{join}}$) elevates zero-label performance to $\mathbf{85.34\%}$ \textbf{Top-1} ($\mathbf{99/116}$), $\mathbf{98.28\%}$ \textbf{Top-2} ($\mathbf{114/116}$), median Q-Error $=1.116$ (P90 $=1.482$), $\mathbf{14.27\text{ ms}}$ execution ($\mathbf{18.42\text{ ms}}$ net), $\mathbf{8.14\%}$ mean relative regret ($34.80\%$ P95), and $\mathbf{0.62\text{ ms}}$ mean absolute regret ($3.34\text{ ms}$ P95 abs; $12.93\%$ abstention). Finally, \textbf{Regime~3 (Supervised 5-Fold Nested CV, In-Domain Upper Bound)} reaches $\mathbf{97.41\%}$ \textbf{Top-1} ($\mathbf{113/116}$), $\mathbf{99.14\%}$ \textbf{Top-2} ($\mathbf{115/116}$), median Q-Error $=1.074$, $\mathbf{13.98\text{ ms}}$ execution ($\mathbf{18.13\text{ ms}}$ net vs.\ $13.83\text{ ms}$ Oracle), and $\mathbf{1.28\%}$ mean relative regret ($\mathbf{0.15\text{ ms}}$ mean absolute regret).
\end{enumerate}

\begin{table*}[!htbp]

\subsubsection{Engine Configuration Manifest, Fair Guarded Baselines, Prospective Holdout, and 213-Query Fairness Protocol}
\label{sec:v852_reviewer_audit}

To ensure strict reproducibility across execution backends~\cite{raasveldt2018fair} and maintain configuration parity across DuckDB, Polars, and DataFusion, all local benchmarks enforce an explicit, version-pinned execution manifest on the primary workstation (Node 1: 8 physical threads, 16\,GB DDR4 RAM, NVIDIA GeForce RTX 2050 4.0\,GB VRAM on AC power with randomized engine interleaving):
\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{DuckDB, Polars, and DataFusion Explicit Configuration:} On Node 1, DuckDB v2.0.0 (cross-checked against the version-pinned cross-ISA parity environment \texttt{duckdb==1.4.5} in Additional File~1, Table~S11) is initialized via \texttt{PRAGMA threads=8; SET memory\_limit='9GB'; SET preserve\_insertion\_order=false;} with temporary spill mapped to local NVMe storage. Polars v2.1.0~\cite{vink2024polars} (cross-checked against \texttt{polars==1.36.1}) is executed with \texttt{POLARS\_MAX\_THREADS=8} over pre-materialized Apache Arrow tables via lazy \texttt{SQLContext(...).execute(q).collect()} (streaming enabled at $\text{SF}\ge 10\text{ GB}$), and Apache DataFusion v55.0.0 (cross-checked against \texttt{datafusion==50.1.0}) is configured with \texttt{target\_partitions=8} and matching $9.0\text{ GB}$ memory pool caps. While DuckDB is the fastest single engine on the 22 official TPC-H queries (\textbf{Q1--Q22}: $22.82\text{ ms}$ mean vs.\ $42.79\text{ ms}$ on Polars, $1.88\times$ faster; $\mathcal{R}_{\text{GBR-9D}}$ achieves $16.98\text{ ms}$, a $1.34\times$ speedup over DuckDB as the best single engine), its higher mean latency on the 75-query factorial workload ($183.74\text{ ms}$ vs.\ $82.47\text{ ms}$ on Polars) stems specifically from multi-way string/date hash joins in Tier L2 ($264.53\text{ ms}$) and staged array \texttt{UNNEST} materialization in Tier L5 ($403.27\text{ ms}$).
    \item \textbf{PyTorch CUDA Relational Tensor Prototype Scope:} The GPU backend is a custom PyTorch~\cite{paszke2019pytorch} CUDA relational tensor prototype operating on contiguous VRAM buffers on a laptop RTX 2050 ($4.0\text{ GB}$ VRAM, $3.2\text{ GB}$ usable guard) rather than a full commercial GPU SQL DBMS. It supports projection, compound boolean filtering, inner equijoins, grouped aggregations, segmented window ranking, and array slicing with verified multiset equivalence ($\text{EX}=1.000$ within $10^{-5}$ FP64 tolerance).
    \item \textbf{Cross-Scale Speedup vs.\ Best Single Engine (Always GPU Included at All Scales):} Reporting speedups strictly against the \emph{best single engine at each scale} rather than the slowest CPU baseline shows that $\mathcal{R}_{\text{GBR-9D}}$~\cite{friedman2001greedy,pedregosa2011scikit} achieves $1.04\times$ at $\text{SF}=1\text{ GB}$ ($54.92\text{ ms}$ net [$50.78\text{ ms}$ exec] canonical $12\%$ vs.\ Always GPU $56.96\text{ ms}$, seed-cluster $p_{\text{seed}}=0.184$, $p_{\text{non-inf}}=0.00067$; and $\mathbf{55.37\text{ ms}}$ net [$51.22\text{ ms}$ exec] under Nested-CV $8\%$ guard, two-sided $\pm 5.0\text{ ms}$ TOST equivalence $p_{\text{TOST}}=0.0442$; $1.50\times$ vs.\ Best CPU Polars $82.47\text{ ms}$, $p_{\text{Holm,seed}}=0.0171$), $1.07\times$ at $\text{SF}=10\text{ GB}$ ($182.40\text{ ms}$ vs.\ Always GPU $195.03\text{ ms}$, $p_{\text{seed}}=0.0412$), $1.07\times$ at $\text{SF}=30\text{ GB}$ ($448.65\text{ ms}$ vs.\ Always GPU $481.85\text{ ms}$, $p_{\text{seed}}=0.0385$), and $1.05\times$ at $\text{SF}=100\text{ GB}$ same-scale ($1142.80\text{ ms}$ vs.\ Always GPU chunked $1199.60\text{ ms}$; under strict Leave-One-Scale-Out extrapolation in Additional File~1 (Table~S9), $\mathcal{R}_{\text{GBR-9D}}$ attains $1214.50\text{ ms}$, trailing Always GPU's $1199.60\text{ ms}$ by $+1.24\%$ [$0.99\times$, $p_{\text{seed}}=0.412$] while outperforming Polars's $6551.42\text{ ms}$ by $5.39\times$ and DuckDB's $8699.98\text{ ms}$ by $7.16\times$). Thus, the primary operational contribution over Always GPU at $\text{SF}=1\text{ GB}$ is \textbf{tail-regret compression} ($2.17\times\text{--}2.25\times$ lower P95 regret: $118.40\%$ nested-CV / $114.11\%$ canonical vs.\ $257.31\%$ on Always GPU; full-workload paired Wilcoxon $p_{\text{raw,seed}}=0.0098, p_{\text{Holm,seed}}=0.0196$ across all $N=25$ seeds [$75$ queries] and $p=0.0042$ on the $n=24$ seeds where routing diverges) while maintaining practical mean-latency equivalence.
    \item \textbf{Prospective Frozen-Model Holdout (Seed S6) and Monetary/Energy Regret:} Freezing all model weights prior to instantiating an unseen 15-query prospective seed cluster (\texttt{S6} across L1--L5 $\times$ V1--V3) under randomized engine interleaving~\cite{raasveldt2018fair} achieves $73.33\%$ Top-1 ($86.67\%$ Top-2, $18.45\%$ mean regret, SHA-256: \texttt{0b85ae0784fd0756...}). Expressing routing regret in physical energy and financial terms confirms that $\mathcal{R}_{\text{GBR-9D}}$ reduces active device energy regret per query from $5.422\text{ J}$ (Always DuckDB) and $2.195\text{ J}$ (Always Polars) down to $0.406\text{ J}$ ($1.381\text{ J}$ total vs.\ $0.975\text{ J}$ Oracle), while maintaining $\$0.00$ marginal cloud scan charges at $\text{SF}=1\text{ GB}$ (vs.\ $\$1,267.66/\text{1M}$ queries on BigQuery).
\end{itemize}


To isolate whether performance gains stem from \textbf{AST-Route}'s routing methodology or from backend implementation differences between SQL engines and the custom PyTorch CUDA tensor prototype, Additional File~1 (Table~S17) reports a three-part Benchmark Fairness Protocol:
(1) \emph{Direct Native-SQL Pool Isolation ($\mathcal{E}_{\text{SQL-Only}}$, $0\%$ Tensor Operations)}: When the author-developed PyTorch CUDA tensor backend is completely excluded, \textbf{AST-Route} routing strictly across native SQL engines (\texttt{DuckDB}, \texttt{Polars SQL}, \texttt{DataFusion SQL}, \texttt{BigQuery}, and \texttt{SQLite3}) achieves $\mathbf{69.12\text{ ms}}$ mean latency ($74.67\%$ Top-1, $96.00\%$ Top-2, $9.64\%$ mean regret, $42.80\%$ P95 regret on Node~1; $\mathbf{62.05\text{ ms}} / 86.67\%$ Top-1 on 22-thread Node~2 $N=30$; and $\mathbf{35.11\text{ ms}} / 97.33\%$ Top-1 on Node~2 $N=75$), significantly outperforming the fastest single SQL engine (\texttt{Always Polars SQL}, $82.47\text{ ms}$, $p_{\text{Holm,seed}}=0.0084$) by $1.19\times$ in latency and $2.77\times$ in P95 tail regret ($118.50\%\rightarrow 42.80\%$), \texttt{Always DataFusion SQL} ($96.30\text{ ms}$) by $1.39\times$, and \texttt{Always DuckDB} ($183.74\text{ ms}$) by $2.66\times$;
(2) \emph{Full Level-3 SQL-to-Tensor Transpilation Penalty}: When every GPU-routed query is explicitly charged with the full $+4.15\text{ ms}$ \texttt{SQLGlot} AST-to-tensor transpilation overhead ($61.11\text{ ms}$ GPU mean vs.\ $56.96\text{ ms}$ Level-2), \textbf{AST-Route} achieves $\mathbf{59.07\text{ ms}}$ net latency ($52.00\%$ Top-1, $120.60\%$ P95 regret vs.\ $285.15\%$ on Always GPU Full-SQL, $p_{\text{seed}}=0.0429$); and
(3) \emph{Common Cold On-Disk ZSTD Parquet Input Boundary}: When all local engines read identical cold on-disk ZSTD Parquet files (\texttt{DuckDB Parquet} $254.42\text{ ms}$, \texttt{Polars Parquet} $116.88\text{ ms}$, \texttt{DataFusion Parquet} $134.18\text{ ms}$, \texttt{GPU Full SQL-Disk} $131.79\text{ ms}$) and \textbf{AST-Route} uses cold NVMe footer seeks ($+4.39\text{ ms}$), \textbf{AST-Route} achieves $\mathbf{113.74\text{ ms}}$ net latency ($61.33\%$ Top-1, $105.28\%$ P95 regret), beating the best single cold-disk engine (\texttt{Polars Parquet}) by $\mathbf{1.03\times}$ ($p_{\text{seed}}=0.0840$) and \texttt{GPU Full SQL-Disk} by $\mathbf{1.16\times}$.

\phantomsection\label{sec:v9_sec3_3_consistency_audit}\label{sec:v9_sec3_4_csci_generalizability_audit}\label{sec:v9_roadmap_v86_closure}

\section{Limitations and Threats to Validity}
To ensure a rigorous interpretation of our findings in accordance with empirical systems engineering standards, we categorize the limitations and threats to validity across four formal validity dimensions:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.5em,itemsep=2pt]
    \item \textbf{Internal Validity (Author-Developed GPU Backend Neutrality, Common I/O Boundaries, and Independent Reproducibility):} Because the PyTorch CUDA relational tensor prototype was implemented by the author (modeling core scan, radix hash-join, segmented group-by, bitonic sort, window prefix-scan, and array unnest primitives within a $4.0\text{ GB}$ RTX~2050 VRAM ceiling via $4\times\text{--}40\times$ PyArrow-to-VRAM chunked streaming rather than a server-grade GPU SQL DBMS such as HeavyDB~\cite{root2019mapd} or Sirius~\cite{yogatama2025sirius}), a natural internal-validity concern is whether routing gains depend on backend implementation differences. We mitigate this threat through three independent controls: (i) evaluating \textbf{Pure Native-SQL Routing ($\mathcal{E}_{\text{SQL-Only}}$, $0\%$ author-developed GPU prototype)} across third-party engines (\texttt{DuckDB}, \texttt{Polars}, \texttt{DataFusion}, \texttt{BigQuery}, \texttt{SQLite~3}), which achieves $69.12\text{ ms}$ ($74.67\%$ Top-1, $p_{\text{Holm}}=0.0084$) on Node~1, $35.11\text{ ms}$ ($97.33\%$ Top-1) on 22-thread Node~2, $29.44\text{ ms}$ ($96.67\%$ Top-1) on Apple M1 Node~3, and $82.76\%$ Top-1 ($98.28\%$ Top-2) zero-shot across all $116$ external queries; (ii) charging GPU-routed queries with full Level-3 SQL-to-tensor transpilation ($+4.15\text{ ms}$, $61.11\text{ ms}$ GPU mean) and evaluating a common cold on-disk ZSTD Parquet boundary ($113.74\text{ ms}$ routed vs.\ $116.88\text{ ms}$ Polars and $131.79\text{ ms}$ GPU); and (iii) providing a one-command clean-room third-party verification suite (\texttt{reproducibility/verify\_\allowbreak{}and\_\allowbreak{}regenerate\_\allowbreak{}main\_\allowbreak{}tables\_\allowbreak{}R1\_\allowbreak{}R6.py}) backed by $180$ SHA-256-verified raw execution logs.
    \item \textbf{Construct Validity (Pre-Execution Overhead Regimes, CSCI Collinearity Control, and CPU/GPU Energy Telemetry Scope):} Construct validity of net routing latency requires separating cache regimes: full cold-SQL parsing with warm OS metadata ($4.15\text{ ms}$; $4.39\text{ ms}$ with cold NVMe \texttt{seek(-8)} footer reads), normalized AST-template caching with live 5-model GBR inference ($0.67\text{ ms}$), and pre-compiled 1D selectivity threshold lookup ($0.08\text{ ms}$). Similarly, while $\text{CSCI}(q)$ is a deterministic linear functional of six AST operators, its construct validity as an oblique tree-splitting feature is verified by orthogonalizing against tree size ($\text{VIF}_{D,N}=1.84$, Model~M5 in Table~\ref{tab:v9_sec3_4_csci_m1_m4_generalizability}). For energy telemetry, while GPU board energy ($E_{\text{GPU}}$) is measured directly via physical NVIDIA NVML sensors ($\text{MAE}=0.008\text{ J}$, $\text{MACE}=1.24\%$), our CPU energy figures ($E_{\text{CPU}}$) on Windows represent \emph{Estimated CPU Package Energy} derived from TDP-calibrated utilization polling (evaluated across $R=50$ repetitions per tier against a non-linear DVFS curve with $\text{MAE}=0.098\text{--}0.205\text{ J}$, $\text{MACE}=3.09\%\text{--}3.21\%$, and Bland--Altman 95\% LoA within $\pm 0.42\text{ J}$) rather than ring-0 Intel RAPL / AMD SMU hardware counters or external wall-socket power meters. Accordingly, all energy optimizations in AST-Route are framed as \emph{energy-aware routing} over estimated CPU package and measured GPU board telemetry, with a four-step Linux ring-0 RAPL (\texttt{/sys/class/powercap/intel-rapl:0/energy\_uj}) calibration protocol provided for bare-metal server deployments.
    \item \textbf{External Validity (153 Unseen Holdout Queries, Skewed String Predicates, Unseen Operators, and Bounded $4\text{ GB}$ PCIe GPU Envelope):} While our core router is trained on $75$ TPC-H queries, external generalization is validated across $\mathbf{153}$ strictly unseen holdout queries ($15$ prospective Seed~\texttt{S6} queries [$73.33\%$ Top-1], $22$ official multi-join TPC-H Q1--Q22 queries [$90.91\%$ Top-2], and $116$ external cross-schema queries across 33 IMDB JOB~\cite{leis2015job}, 13 SSB~\cite{oneil2009ssb}, 20 ClickBench~\cite{clickbench2022}, 30 TPC-DS-derived~\cite{nambiar2006tpcds}, and 20 BIRD-inspired financial queries~\cite{li2023bird}). Nonetheless, three explicit external scope boundaries govern our findings: (i) min/max zone maps cannot capture unanchored substring predicates (\texttt{LIKE '\%...\%'}) or Zipfian join skew on IMDB JOB ($21.21\%$ zero-shot 9D Top-1, though conformal abstention selects Polars at $6.75\text{ ms}$ with $100\%$ Top-2 and our $10.18\text{ KB}$ 12D catalog sketch elevates Top-1 to $69.70\%$ [$4.54\text{ ms}$] on JOB and $85.34\%$ across all $116$ external queries); (ii) under Leave-One-Tier-Out CV for unseen operator families, unguarded tree regressors degrade ($85.42\text{ ms}$), requiring our structural OOD gate ($100\%$ recall, restoring $76.18\text{ ms}$); and (iii) on the hardware axis, while we validated cross-ISA CPU routing across three physical machines (\texttt{x86\_64} 8-thread Node~1, \texttt{x86\_64} 22-thread Node~2, and Apple M1 \texttt{ARM64} Node~3) and concurrent streams up to $C=64$ ($154.44\text{ QPS}$ peak at $C=32$), all empirical GPU PCIe crossover thresholds ($\Phi_{\text{PCIe}}$) and $1\times\text{--}40\times$ chunked streaming laws are strictly scoped to our tested $4.0\text{ GB}$ PCIe Gen4 RTX~2050 discrete GPU envelope and cannot be extrapolated without empirical re-calibration to data-center GPUs (e.g., H100/B200 with NVLink/HBM3e) or distributed multi-node engines (ClickHouse~\cite{schulze2024clickhouse}, Trino~\cite{sethi2019presto}).
    \item \textbf{Statistical Conclusion Validity (TOST Practical Equivalence vs.\ Null Superiority, Full-Workload vs.\ Divergent-Seed Regret Tests, and Hyperparameter Sensitivity):} Because an unpowered two-sided superiority test ($p_{\text{seed}}=0.184$ vs.\ Always GPU) cannot prove latency equivalence, we establish statistical conclusion validity via formal Paired Two One-Sided Tests (TOST) within $\pm\Delta_{\text{eq}}=\pm 5.0\text{ ms}$ (two-sided $p_{\text{TOST}}=0.0442 < 0.05$, $p_{\text{non-inf}}=0.0012$ under nested-CV $\hat{\tau}_{\text{guard}}^{(-k)}=8\%$ at $55.37\text{ ms}$ net; one-sided non-inferiority $p_{\text{non-inf}}=0.00067$ and $\pm 6.0\text{ ms}$ TOST $p_{\text{TOST},\pm 6\text{ms}}=0.0241$ under canonical $\tau_{\text{guard}}=12\%$ at $54.92\text{ ms}$ net). Similarly, to avoid selection bias in tail-regret inference, we report both the full-workload $N=25$ seed-cluster Wilcoxon signed-rank test ($p_{\text{raw,seed}}=0.0098, p_{\text{Holm,seed}}=0.0196$ across all $75$ queries) and the $n=24$ divergent-seed test ($p_{\text{seed}}=0.0042$), alongside 10,000-iteration paired bootstrap 95\% CIs, sample-size convergence ($K \in \{5,\dots,25\}$ reaching $>86\%$ power), and joint hyperparameter sweeps across $\tau_{\text{guard}} \in [0\%, 30\%]$ and conformal $\alpha_{\text{conf}} \in [0.05, 0.20]$.
\end{enumerate}


\section{Conclusion}
We presented AST-Route, an interpretable, risk-aware multi-objective query routing framework grounded in empirical CPU--GPU--cloud crossover profiling and evaluated across 213 benchmark SQL queries (228 including 15 prospective Seed~\texttt{S6} holdouts)---comprising 97 core TPC-H benchmark queries (75 AST-stratified factorial queries across L1--L5 and V1--V3 plus the 22 official TPC-H queries Q1--Q22~\cite{tpc2022tpch}) and 116 external cross-schema queries across five suites (33 IMDB JOB~\cite{leis2015job}, 13 SSB~\cite{oneil2009ssb}, 20 ClickBench~\cite{clickbench2022}, 30 TPC-DS-derived snowflake~\cite{nambiar2006tpcds}, and 20 BIRD-inspired financial queries~\cite{li2023bird})---across four scale factors ($\text{SF}=1, 10, 30, 100\text{ GB}$), three physical hardware nodes (\texttt{x86\_64}+GPU Node~1, 22-thread \texttt{x86\_64} Node~2, and Apple M1 \texttt{ARM64} Node~3), $C=1\text{--}64$ concurrent query streams, and six software engines across four computing paradigms (SQLite~3, Google BigQuery, DuckDB, Polars, Apache DataFusion, and CUDA GPU). Validated via 10,000-iteration seed-clustered bootstrap 95\% CIs, TOST equivalence, and leak-free 25-seed LOSO-CV, our pre-execution 9D/10D representation ($\mathbf{x}_q\in\mathbb{R}^9$, $\mathbf{x}_q^{\text{scale}}\in\mathbb{R}^{10}$) predicts multi-engine cost (median Q-Error $1.007\text{--}1.172$, $R^2=0.815\text{--}0.875$) across three overhead regimes ($4.15\text{ ms}$ cold SQL, $0.67\text{ ms}$ AST cache, $0.08\text{ ms}$ 1D threshold cache). While unguarded point-estimate routers (Ridge $49.12\text{ ms}$ exec / $52.77\text{ ms}$ net; shallow DT $51.35\text{ ms}$ exec / $55.00\text{ ms}$ net) suffer elevated P95 tail regret ($150.9\%\text{--}196.8\%$) and guarded linear/tree baselines reach $55.49\text{--}56.55\text{ ms}$ net ($128.4\%\text{--}138.5\%$ P95), margin-guarded AST-Route ($\mathcal{R}_{\text{GBR-9D}}$) achieves $55.37\text{ ms}$ net under nested-CV ($51.22\text{ ms}$ exec, $118.40\%$ P95 regret, two-sided $\pm 5.0\text{ ms}$ TOST $p_{\text{TOST}}=0.0442$) and $54.92\text{ ms}$ net under canonical $\tau_{\text{guard}}=12\%$ ($50.78\text{ ms}$ exec, $57.33\%$ Top-1, $76.00\%$ Top-2, $0.00\%$ median regret, $1.381\text{ J}$ device energy, $p_{\text{non-inf}}=0.00067$ vs.\ Always GPU at $56.96\text{ ms}$), cutting P95 tail regret by $2.17\times\text{--}2.25\times$ ($118.40\% / 114.11\%$ [$35.90 / 34.51\text{ ms}$] vs.\ $257.31\%$ [$51.42\text{ ms}$], full-workload $p_{\text{raw,seed}}=0.0098, p_{\text{Holm,seed}}=0.0196$; $n=24$ $p_{\text{seed}}=0.0042$) and outperforming static CPUs by $1.50\times\text{--}3.35\times$ ($p_{\text{Holm,seed}}\le 0.0171$). Pure Native-SQL routing ($M_{\text{gpu\_max}}=0$, capturing $48.5\%$ of the 6-engine routed gain) achieves $69.12\text{ ms}$ ($74.67\%$ Top-1, $p_{\text{Holm}}=0.0084$) on Node~1, $35.11\text{ ms}$ ($97.33\%$ Top-1) on Node~2, $29.44\text{ ms}$ ($96.67\%$ Top-1) on Apple M1 Node~3, and $82.76\%$ Top-1 ($98.28\%$ Top-2) zero-shot across all 116 external queries ($69.83\%$ strict 9D and $85.34\%$ 12D sketch across all engines), alongside $16.98\text{ ms}$ ($1.34\times$ vs.\ DuckDB) on TPC-H Q1--Q22, $154.44\text{ QPS}$ peak throughput at $C=32$ ($2.14\times$), and $1.04\times\text{--}1.07\times$ speedups over the best single engine across $\text{SF}=1\text{--}100\text{ GB}$. Synthesizing our findings establishes three methodological conclusions: (i) \textit{Oblique Structural Projection and Tail-Risk Control}: $\text{CSCI}(q)$ ($\text{VIF}_{D,N}=1.84$) prunes $-28.2\%$ of depth-3 tree splits and lowers out-of-fold regret by $-9.22\text{ pp}$ ($p=0.0038$, capturing $52.1\%$ of the $8\text{D}\rightarrow 9\text{D}$ feature gain and $16.5\%$ of the M1-to-oracle gap), while complexity-weighted Mondrian conformal bounds ($90.93\%$ coverage, $\text{ECE}=0.67\%$) protect tail SLAs near PCIe crossover boundaries; (ii) \textit{Operator-Bounded Cross-Workload Transfer}: AST-Route generalizes zero-shot to star/snowflake schemas (SSB $92.31\%$, ClickBench $80.00\%$, TPC-DS-derived $93.33\%$, BIRD-inspired $90.00\%$ Top-1), while mitigating string-skewed joins (JOB $69.70\%$ Top-1 via $10.18\text{ KB}$ 12D sketches) and unseen operator families via our structural OOD fallback gate ($76.18\text{ ms}$ on LOTO-CV); and (iii) \textit{Multi-Scale and Multi-Objective Dispatch}: coupling zone-map selectivity with catalog byte footprints and hardware descriptors ($\mathbf{h}_{\text{hw}}$) enables seamless orchestration across in-VRAM GPU execution ($\text{SF}=1\text{ GB}$), chunked PCIe streaming ($\text{SF}=10\text{--}30\text{ GB}$), and serverless cloud spill offloading ($\text{SF}=100\text{ GB}$, saving $1.50\text{ hr}/1\text{k}$ queries vs.\ CPU or $\$507.06/1\text{M}$ under FinOps constraints).

\section*{Declarations}

\subsection*{Abbreviations}
AST: Abstract Syntax Tree; B-Tree: Balanced Tree; CI: Confidence Interval; CPU: Central Processing Unit; CSCI: Composite Structural Complexity Index; CTE: Common Table Expression; CV: Coefficient of Variation; CUDA: Compute Unified Device Architecture; DBMS: Database Management System; DT: Decision Tree; EX: Execution Accuracy; FinOps: Cloud Financial Operations; FP64: 64-Bit Double-Precision Floating-Point; GBR: Gradient Boosting Regressor; GCS: Google Cloud Storage; GPU: Graphics Processing Unit; H2D: Host-to-Device; I/O: Input/Output; JOB: Join Order Benchmark; LOO-CV: Leave-One-Query-Out Cross-Validation; LOOO: Leave-One-Operator-Out; LOSO-CV: Leave-One-Seed-Out Cross-Validation; MAPE: Mean Absolute Percentage Error; MC: Monte Carlo; MPP: Massively Parallel Processing; NVML: NVIDIA Management Library; OLAP: Online Analytical Processing; PCIe: Peripheral Component Interconnect Express; QPS: Queries Per Second; RAM: Random-Access Memory; RF: Random Forest; SARGable: Search Argument Able; SF: Scale Factor; SIMD: Single Instruction, Multiple Data; SIMT: Single Instruction, Multiple Threads; SQL: Structured Query Language; SSB: Star Schema Benchmark; TOST: Two One-Sided Tests; TPC-H: Transaction Processing Performance Council Ad-Hoc Decision Support Benchmark; VES: Valid Efficiency Score; VRAM: Video Random-Access Memory; ZSTD: Zstandard Compression.

\subsection*{Ethics approval and consent to participate}
Not applicable. This study utilizes synthetic benchmark data generated via the industry-standard TPC-H benchmark specification \cite{tpc2022tpch} and does not involve human participants, animal subjects, or personally identifiable data. In accordance with the TPC Fair-Use Policy, all workloads and metrics in this study represent non-official, TPC-H-derived research evaluations and are not comparable to official audited TPC benchmark results.

\subsection*{Consent for publication}
Not applicable.

\subsection*{Availability of data and materials}
All multi-scale TPC-H data generation scripts ($\text{SF}=1, 10, 30$, and $100\text{ GB}$), the 75-query AST-stratified SQL suite (25 canonical seeds $\times$ 3 selectivity horizons across tiers L1--L5), official TPC-H Q1--Q22, IMDB JOB ($N=33$), SSB ($N=13$), ClickBench ($N=20$), TPC-DS-derived ($N=30$), and BIRD-inspired synthetic financial ($N=20$) evaluation harnesses, raw per-trial execution and hardware/energy telemetry CSV logs across all three physical nodes (\texttt{x86\_64}+RTX~2050, 22-thread \texttt{x86\_64}, and Apple M1 \texttt{ARM64}), clean-room Docker lockfiles (\texttt{reproducibility/\allowbreak{}Dockerfile} and \texttt{requirements-\allowbreak{}lock.txt}), Additional File~1 (Supplementary Appendix), and our one-command independent reproducibility and main-table regeneration script (\texttt{python reproducibility/verify\_\allowbreak{}and\_\allowbreak{}regenerate\_\allowbreak{}main\_\allowbreak{}tables\_\allowbreak{}R1\_\allowbreak{}R6.py}, executing Verification Protocols \textbf{R1--R6} with $0.000$ maximum numeric discrepancy across all manuscript tables) are publicly available in the active open-source repository at \url{https://github.com/preecha-msu/bigquery-olap-benchmark}. Complete 64-character SHA-256 checksums for all raw telemetry, reanalysis, cross-ISA isolation, stability, and calibration artifacts---anchored by primary routing evaluation \texttt{89b1ec64cca15b2dd41a4ad2...}, AST profile \texttt{4cb5f7477fd24dc77a6fd2b5...}, version-pinned parity \texttt{f12ae28467f9802a2967...}, and 3-node global registry \texttt{5adfa064816f883f8770...}---are indexed in \texttt{results/\allowbreak{}global\_\allowbreak{}sha256\_\allowbreak{}registry.csv} for single-command verification. Each reported aggregate metric is linked to its protocol identifier, dataset checksum, software version, hardware manifest, and execution-boundary definition.

The author read and approved the final manuscript.
